        Idea:
# Sprint brief — Tickets MVP

## Goal

Implement the tickets feature in megaplan per the design in `docs/tickets.md`. Tickets are short, repo-scoped notes on issues/problems, captured by humans or by agents on a human's prompt, that get folded into epics and auto-addressed when the resolving epic completes.

**The design doc `docs/tickets.md` is the source of truth for the data model, CLI surface, file format, modes, codebase identity, and integration touchpoints. Read it first. This brief only adds sprint scoping — what's in, what's out, what success looks like.**

## In scope for this sprint

Everything required to make tickets usable end-to-end in both **local-only mode** and **cloud-configured mode**:

1. **Schema migration (cloud-only)**: add `tickets` and `ticket_epics` tables; add `root_commit_sha` column to `codebases` with an index. Additive only — no changes to existing tables beyond the new column.
2. **`megaplan/store/db.py`**: CRUD helpers for the new tables, column allowlist entries, codebase resolution that prefers `root_commit_sha`.
3. **New module `megaplan/tickets/`**: the canonical local representation. Frontmatter read/write for `.megaplan/tickets/{ulid}-{slug}.md` files. Mode-aware dispatch (cloud-configured → file + DB; local-only → file only). Codebase identity resolution from cwd via `git rev-list --max-parents=0 HEAD`.
4. **CLI `megaplan ticket …`** with all verbs in `docs/tickets.md` § CLI: `new`, `list`, `show`, `edit`, `link`, `unlink`, `addressed`, `dismiss`, `reopen`. `ticket new` prints only the ULID to stdout on success; `--json` on read commands. `source` auto-derived from `MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID` env.
5. **Auto-address hook**: in the epic state-transition path, after an epic moves to `done`, call `megaplan.tickets.address_resolved_by_epic(epic_id)`. Implementation handles both modes per the design doc. Idempotent.
6. **Planner discovery integration**: when assembling context for new/refined epics, include open tickets for the current repo, ranked by `tag overlap with epic goal desc, created_at desc`. The planner can propose links with `resolves_on_complete=true`. Same data shape in both modes (DB query in cloud, file scan in local-only).
7. **Resident/agent prompt**: a short paragraph teaching the agent that `megaplan ticket new` exists and when to use it (out-of-scope observations during epic work, user-requested captures).
8. **Tests** covering both modes for: file ↔ DB round-trip, link/unlink, auto-address idempotence, codebase identity resolution (rename case, fresh-repo case), CLI happy paths.

## Out of scope (defer)

- **Offline queue / `.pending/` directory / `megaplan ticket sync`**. Write-through only for now; if the DB write fails in cloud mode, the CLI errors loudly and the user retries. The design doc describes the offline queue — explicitly **do not build it** this sprint. Build the minimal write-through path.
- Conflict detection on concurrent edits — last-write-wins is fine.
- Severity, assignment, comments, cross-repo tickets — explicitly excluded per the design doc.
- Anything described in the doc's **Open items** section.

## Success criteria

- `megaplan ticket new "title" -b "body"` works in a local-only repo and writes the `.md` file.
- The same command in a cloud-configured repo writes the `.md` file **and** inserts a `tickets` row, auto-registering the codebase if needed (with `root_commit_sha` populated).
- `megaplan ticket link <ticket> <epic> --resolves` updates both the file frontmatter and the DB join row.
- Marking an epic `done` flips the linked ticket's status to `addressed` and writes a `resolution_note` referencing the epic — in both modes.
- New epic creation surfaces open tickets for the repo in the planner's context, ranked.
- Tests pass for both modes.

## Notes for the planner

- Follow the **core-first principle**: `megaplan/tickets/` owns the capability; the DB layer is purely additive and only kicks in when a store is configured. Mode detection follows whatever mechanism megaplan core already uses (presence of a configured store) — do not invent a new one.
- The `tickets` concept is **distinct from `feedback`**. Do not reuse the `feedback` table; do not conflate their lifecycles.
- Vendor flags / FK conventions / migration patterns should match how existing tables are handled in `megaplan/store/db.py`. Read existing CRUD helpers before designing new ones.
- The auto-address hook integration point is the one place where you must touch existing epic state-transition code. Find the existing hook surface (where epic events are emitted on state change) and add the call there — don't invent a parallel transition path.

        Current plan (markdown):
        # Implementation Plan: Tickets MVP

## Overview
Add a repo-scoped tickets feature per `docs/tickets.md`. Build a new `megaplan/tickets/` module that owns the canonical local representation, wire mode-aware dispatch into the DB store, register a `megaplan ticket ...` CLI subcommand group, and inject one auto-address call into the place where epic state actually transitions to `done`. DB changes are additive: new `tickets` and `ticket_epics` tables plus a `root_commit_sha` column on `codebases`. No `.pending/` queue this sprint.

Verified repo facts (corrections from prior plan):
- `update_epic` (`megaplan/store/db.py:584-614`, `megaplan/store/file.py:729-744`) is a generic UPDATE on arbitrary `**changes` and has no `state == 'done'` branch today. The prior `:960`/`:1054` lines were inside `update_checklist_item`.
- The Epic state column is `state` (not `status`).
- Epic state transitions are recorded via `store.record_epic_event` (`db.py:2295`, called from `megaplan/editorial/_events.py:63` and `megaplan/editorial/gating.py:213`).
- `create_codebase`/`upsert_codebase` INSERTs (`db.py:1797-1811`, `db.py:1830+`) hard-enumerate columns; allowlist alone will not persist `root_commit_sha`.
- No `cloud_configured` predicate exists in `_core` today. Plan defines `megaplan.tickets.is_cloud_store(store) := isinstance(store, DBStore)` as the canonical signal.
- `MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID` is not exported by the agent runtime today; the resident loop must export it for `source='agent'` to function.

## Phase 1: Schema + Types

### Step 1: Audit (`megaplan/store/db.py`, `megaplan/store/file.py`, `megaplan/cli.py`, `megaplan/handlers/plan.py`, `megaplan/editorial/_events.py`, `megaplan/editorial/gating.py`, `megaplan/resident/runtime.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Read codebase CRUD (`db.py:1779-1955`) and allowlists (`db.py:237-238`).
2. Map every code path that drives an epic to `state='done'`: `update_epic`, `record_epic_event`, editorial gating call sites, and any wrappers in `megaplan/store/multi.py`/`megaplan/store/compat.py`.
3. Locate the resident subprocess-spawn site in `megaplan/resident/runtime.py:167` for env wiring.
4. Skim `cli.py:1256` (`epic` subparser) for the established pattern.
5. Locate the planner context-assembly site in `megaplan/handlers/plan.py`.

### Step 2: Schema migration (`supabase/migrations/2026MMDDHHMM_tickets.sql`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Additive migration: ALTER `codebases` ADD COLUMN `root_commit_sha text` + index; CREATE TABLE `tickets` and CREATE TABLE `ticket_epics` per the design doc.
2. FK column types are explicitly `text` for actors/turns/epics references; `codebases.id` remains `uuid`.

### Step 3: Schema dataclasses (`megaplan/schemas/models.py`, `megaplan/schemas/__init__.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Add `Ticket` and `TicketEpicLink` models; export.
2. Add `root_commit_sha: str | None = None` to `Codebase`.

## Phase 2: DB Store

### Step 4: DB store CRUD (`megaplan/store/db.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Extend `_IDEMPOTENT_MUTATORS` (`db.py:89`) and exports (`db.py:114`) with the new helper names.
2. Extend column allowlists (`db.py:237-238`): add `root_commit_sha` to `codebases`; add `tickets` and `ticket_epics` entries.
3. **Extend `create_codebase` and `upsert_codebase` INSERT SQL** (`db.py:1797-1811`, `db.py:1830+`) to include `root_commit_sha` in column list, VALUES, and parameter array. Add `root_commit_sha: str | None = None` to both signatures. For `upsert_codebase`, also add to the `ON CONFLICT ... DO UPDATE SET` clause.
4. Implement: `create_ticket`, `load_ticket`, `list_tickets`, `update_ticket` (no revision; LWW), `link_ticket_to_epic` (INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE), `unlink_ticket_from_epic`, `list_ticket_epic_links`, and `address_tickets_resolved_by_epic(epic_id) -> list[str]` (single SQL UPDATE filtered on `resolves_on_complete=true AND status='open'`, RETURNING ids; idempotent).
5. `resolve_codebase_by_root_sha(*, root_sha, owner, name, repo_url, default_branch) -> str`: prefer `root_commit_sha`; fall back to `(owner,name)` and backfill; else create with `root_commit_sha` populated.

## Phase 3: Tickets Module

### Step 5: New module `megaplan/tickets/`
**Scope:** Medium
1. `megaplan/tickets/__init__.py` exports public API plus `is_cloud_store`.
2. `megaplan/tickets/identity.py`: `repo_root_sha` via `git rev-list --max-parents=0 HEAD` (clean error on no-commits; sort-and-pick on multiple roots); `repo_owner_name`; `repo_default_branch`.
3. `megaplan/tickets/files.py`: tickets dir, frontmatter read/write via `pyyaml`, slugify, iterators.
4. `megaplan/tickets/core.py`: **`is_cloud_store(store) -> bool := isinstance(store, DBStore)`** — canonical predicate. `_resolve_store()` reuses megaplan's existing factory. Each public op writes the file first; if cloud, also calls the DB helper. DB error in cloud mode raises loudly. `address_resolved_by_epic(epic_id, *, store=None, repo_root=None)`: `store` is the **resolved tickets/cloud store**, not the epic-event caller's `self`. File path always; cloud path additional.
5. `_derive_source()` reads `MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID` env (set -> agent + filed_in_turn; unset -> human). `MEGAPLAN_ACTOR_ID` populates filed_by_actor_id when present.
6. Stdin body: `new(...)` accepts `body='-'` to read `sys.stdin.read()`; rejects empty.

## Phase 4: CLI + Hooks

### Step 6: CLI subcommand group (`megaplan/cli.py`, `megaplan/handlers/tickets.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Register `ticket` subparser (mirror `epic` block at `cli.py:1256`): `new <title> [-b BODY | --edit | -] [-t TAGS]`, `list [filters] [--json]`, `show`, `edit`, `link [--resolves]`, `unlink`, `addressed`, `dismiss`, `reopen`. Body source is a mutually exclusive required group; `-` reads stdin via `sys.stdin.read()` and rejects empty.
2. `megaplan/handlers/tickets.py` — one handler per verb. `ticket new` writes only ULID + newline to stdout; chatter to stderr.
3. Wire dispatch in `cli.py` router.

### Step 7: Auto-address hook (`megaplan/store/db.py`, `megaplan/store/file.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **In `update_epic`** (`db.py:584`, `file.py:729`), after the row is persisted, gate on `changes.get('state') == 'done'`. When true, call `address_resolved_by_epic(epic_id, store=_resolve_store(), repo_root=os.getcwd())`. The store passed is the resolved tickets/cloud store, not `self`.
2. **In `record_epic_event`** (`db.py:2295`), add the same gated call when the recorded event is a transition to `state='done'` (check `event_type` and `post_state.get('state')`).
3. Audit `megaplan/store/multi.py` and `megaplan/store/compat.py` wrappers; hook there or normalize the key if they reshape `changes['state']`.
4. Idempotency comes from the helper itself.

### Step 8: Planner discovery integration (`megaplan/handlers/plan.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Identify the new/refined-epic context-assembly function.
2. Add an "Open tickets for this repo" section (top 20) ranked **strictly per the brief**: `tag overlap with epic GOAL desc, created_at desc` — overlap source is the epic goal text only, not title. Cloud -> `store.list_tickets(codebase_id, status='open')`; local-only -> `megaplan.tickets.list_open(repo_root)`.
3. Prompt mentions the planner can propose links with `resolves_on_complete=true`; the actual linking happens via the explicit CLI.

### Step 9: Resident/agent wiring + prompt (`megaplan/resident/runtime.py`, `megaplan/data/instructions.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Export `MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID=<turn.id>` (and `MEGAPLAN_ACTOR_ID` when available) in subprocess env at `megaplan/resident/runtime.py:167`.
2. Add a short paragraph to `megaplan/data/instructions.md`: when to use `megaplan ticket new`; ULID is printed to stdout.

## Phase 5: Tests + Validation

### Step 10: Tests (`tests/test_tickets.py`, `tests/test_tickets_cli.py`, extend `tests/test_cli_entry.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Local-only mode: `new`, `new -b -` (stdin), `list`, `show`, `edit`, `link`/`unlink`, status transitions, `address_resolved_by_epic` walks files idempotently.
2. Cloud-configured mode (DBStore fixture): same ops do file + DB write-through; `link --resolves` writes both.
3. Codebase auto-registration: first `ticket new` in a fresh repo creates a `codebases` row with `root_commit_sha` populated **NON-NULL**.
4. Auto-address hook: drive an epic to `state='done'` via `update_epic` AND via the editorial-gating path that calls `record_epic_event`; verify both file and DB flip; second transition is a no-op. A non-`done` `update_epic` call does NOT trigger the hook.
5. Identity: rename case (same root SHA -> same codebase_id); fresh-repo case (no commits -> CLI errors cleanly).
6. CLI happy paths via subprocess: `ticket new` stdout is exactly the ULID + newline; `list --json` valid JSON; `link --resolves` round-trip.

### Step 11: Validation
**Scope:** Small
1. `pytest tests/test_tickets.py tests/test_tickets_cli.py -x`.
2. `pytest tests/test_cli_entry.py tests/test_auto.py -x`.
3. `pytest -x`.
4. Smoke: `MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID=t_test megaplan ticket new "test" -b "body" -t demo` in a scratch repo; verify stdout is just the ULID; file has `source: agent`, `filed_in_turn: t_test`.

## Execution Order
1. Phase 1 (migration + models).
2. Phase 2 (DB CRUD + extended `create_codebase`/`upsert_codebase` INSERTs).
3. Phase 3 (`megaplan/tickets/` with `is_cloud_store`).
4. Phase 4: CLI -> hooks in both `update_epic` and `record_epic_event` -> planner + agent env.
5. Phase 5: tests + validation.

## Validation Order
1. Targeted ticket tests.
2. CLI + epic-transition regression.
3. Full suite.
4. Manual smoke.

  "rationale": "The plan has improved from iteration 1 (score 21.0 \u2192 29.0, delta 85%) but three distinct correctness gaps remain that would produce real bugs at implementation time. (1) The auto-address hook inside `update_epic` passes `repo_root=os.getcwd()`, which would write ticket files to the wrong directory in any server/multi-repo deployment where DBStore serves multiple codebases \u2014 the plan must derive `repo_root` from the epic's `codebase_id` via `codebases.repo_workspace` or an explicit parameter rather than cwd. (2) The `record_epic_event` hook gate on `event_type` is underspecified \u2014 without naming the canonical event_type taxonomy (e.g., `'state_transition'`, `'epic_completed'`), the gate could fire on non-transition events that happen to carry `state='done'` in post_state or miss the actual transition entirely. (3) The `MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID` env-var export targets `megaplan/resident/runtime.py:167`, but evidence from the codebase shows that line is inside an `update_message` call and the runtime uses an in-process async runner with no subprocess spawn; agent subprocesses are launched in `megaplan/workers.py`, and that is where the env export must live for `source='agent'` to function. These are all fixable in one more plan revision. The double-fire concern (both hooks triggering on the same transition) is minor since the operation is idempotent, but the plan should acknowledge it explicitly.",

    "iteration": 2,
    "idea": "# Sprint brief \u2014 Tickets MVP\n\n## Goal\n\nImplement the tickets feature in megaplan per the design in `docs/tickets.md`. Tickets are short, repo-scoped notes on issues/problems, captured by humans or by agents on a human's prompt, that get folded into epics and auto-addressed when the resolving epic completes.\n\n**The design doc `docs/tickets.md` is the source of truth for the data model, CLI surface, file format, modes, codebase identity, and integration touchpoints. Read it first. This brief only adds sprint scoping \u2014 what's in, what's out, what success looks like.**\n\n## In scope for this sprint\n\nEverything required to make tickets usable end-to-end in both **local-only mode** and **cloud-configured mode**:\n\n1. **Schema migration (cloud-only)**: add `tickets` and `ticket_epics` tables; add `root_commit_sha` column to `codebases` with an index. Additive only \u2014 no changes to existing tables beyond the new column.\n2. **`megaplan/store/db.py`**: CRUD helpers for the new tables, column allowlist entries, codebase resolution that prefers `root_commit_sha`.\n3. **New module `megaplan/tickets/`**: the canonical local representation. Frontmatter read/write for `.megaplan/tickets/{ulid}-{slug}.md` files. Mode-aware dispatch (cloud-configured \u2192 file + DB; local-only \u2192 file only). Codebase identity resolution from cwd via `git rev-list --max-parents=0 HEAD`.\n4. **CLI `megaplan ticket \u2026`** with all verbs in `docs/tickets.md` \u00a7 CLI: `new`, `list`, `show`, `edit`, `link`, `unlink`, `addressed`, `dismiss`, `reopen`. `ticket new` prints only the ULID to stdout on success; `--json` on read commands. `source` auto-derived from `MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID` env.\n5. **Auto-address hook**: in the epic state-transition path, after an epic moves to `done`, call `megaplan.tickets.address_resolved_by_epic(epic_id)`. Implementation handles both modes per the design doc. Idempotent.\n6. **Planner discovery integration**: when assembling context for new/refined epics, include open tickets for the current repo, ranked by `tag overlap with epic goal desc, created_at desc`. The planner can propose links with `resolves_on_complete=true`. Same data shape in both modes (DB query in cloud, file scan in local-only).\n7. **Resident/agent prompt**: a short paragraph teaching the agent that `megaplan ticket new` exists and when to use it (out-of-scope observations during epic work, user-requested captures).\n8. **Tests** covering both modes for: file \u2194 DB round-trip, link/unlink, auto-address idempotence, codebase identity resolution (rename case, fresh-repo case), CLI happy paths.\n\n## Out of scope (defer)\n\n- **Offline queue / `.pending/` directory / `megaplan ticket sync`**. Write-through only for now; if the DB write fails in cloud mode, the CLI errors loudly and the user retries. The design doc describes the offline queue \u2014 explicitly **do not build it** this sprint. Build the minimal write-through path.\n- Conflict detection on concurrent edits \u2014 last-write-wins is fine.\n- Severity, assignment, comments, cross-repo tickets \u2014 explicitly excluded per the design doc.\n- Anything described in the doc's **Open items** section.\n\n## Success criteria\n\n- `megaplan ticket new \"title\" -b \"body\"` works in a local-only repo and writes the `.md` file.\n- The same command in a cloud-configured repo writes the `.md` file **and** inserts a `tickets` row, auto-registering the codebase if needed (with `root_commit_sha` populated).\n- `megaplan ticket link <ticket> <epic> --resolves` updates both the file frontmatter and the DB join row.\n- Marking an epic `done` flips the linked ticket's status to `addressed` and writes a `resolution_note` referencing the epic \u2014 in both modes.\n- New epic creation surfaces open tickets for the repo in the planner's context, ranked.\n- Tests pass for both modes.\n\n## Notes for the planner\n\n- Follow the **core-first principle**: `megaplan/tickets/` owns the capability; the DB layer is purely additive and only kicks in when a store is configured. Mode detection follows whatever mechanism megaplan core already uses (presence of a configured store) \u2014 do not invent a new one.\n- The `tickets` concept is **distinct from `feedback`**. Do not reuse the `feedback` table; do not conflate their lifecycles.\n- Vendor flags / FK conventions / migration patterns should match how existing tables are handled in `megaplan/store/db.py`. Read existing CRUD helpers before designing new ones.\n- The auto-address hook integration point is the one place where you must touch existing epic state-transition code. Find the existing hook surface (where epic events are emitted on state change) and add the call there \u2014 don't invent a parallel transition path.",

      {
        "id": "issue_hints-1",
        "concern": "Did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: The brief lists CLI verbs including `ticket new` with `-b/--edit/-` (stdin) input mode.         "concern": "Are the proposed changes technically correct?: are the proposed changes technically correct?: Step 7.1 calls `address_resolved_by_epic(epic_id, store=_resolve_store(), repo_root=os.getcwd())` from inside `update_epic`. Two correctness concerns: (a) `os.getcwd()` inside a store method ties ticket file resolution to wherever the process happens to be running, which is wrong for any server/multi-repo deployment where the DBStore serves multiple codebases \u2014 the file-side update may write to the wrong `.megaplan/tickets/` or none. (b) `_resolve_store()` is invoked from inside a DBStore method to obtain a tickets store; since `is_cloud_store(store) := isinstance(store, DBStore)`, calling `_resolve_store()` recursively from `self`'s own method is at best redundant and at worst a circular dependency on the module-level factory.",

        - Preserve or improve success criteria quality. Each criterion must have a `priority` of `must`, `should`, or `info`. Promote or demote priorities if critique feedback reveals a criterion was over- or under-weighted.
        - Each success criterion should include a `requires` field listing the capabilities needed for verification. Valid capability strings: `run_shell`, `read_files`, `run_tests`, `parse_diff`, `read_build_output`, `run_linter` (container), `drive_browser`, `inspect_runtime_ui`, `observe_runtime_logs`, `subjective_judgment`, `verify_physical_device` (human). `must` criteria MUST have non-empty `requires`. Example: `{"criterion": "All tests pass", "priority": "must", "requires": ["run_tests"]}`.

        - Remove unjustified scope growth. If critique raised scope creep, narrow the plan back to the original idea unless the broader work is strictly required.
        - Maintain the structural template: H1 title, ## Overview, phase sections with numbered step sections, ## Execution Order or ## Validation Order.
        - CRITICAL: Your entire revised plan markdown (all sections) must be output as the `plan` field in the structured output. The prose response must not contain the plan text.

        Plan template — simple format (adapt to the actual repo and scope):
````md
# Implementation Plan: [Title]

## Overview
Summarize the goal, current repository shape, and the constraints that matter.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Audit the current behavior (`megaplan/prompts.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Inspect** the current implementation and call out the exact insertion points (`megaplan/prompts.py:29`).

### Step 2: Add the first change (`megaplan/evaluation.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** the smallest viable change with exact file references (`megaplan/evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Capture** any tricky behavior with a short example.
   ```python
   issues = validate_plan_structure(plan_text)
   ```

### Step 3: Wire downstream behavior (`megaplan/handlers.py`, `megaplan/workers.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Update** the runtime flow in the touched files (`megaplan/handlers.py:400`, `megaplan/workers.py:199`).

### Step 4: Prove the change (`tests/test_evaluation.py`, `tests/test_megaplan.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** the cheapest targeted checks first (`tests/test_evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Finish** with broader verification once the wiring is in place (`tests/test_megaplan.py:1`).

## Execution Order
1. Update prompts and mocks before enforcing stricter validation.
2. Land higher-risk wiring after the validator and tests are ready.

## Validation Order
1. Start with focused unit tests.
2. Run the broader suite after the flow changes are in place.
````

For complex plans, use multiple phases:
````md
## Phase 1: Foundation — Dependencies, DB, Types

### Step 1: Install dependencies (`package.json`)
...

### Step 2: Create database migration (`supabase/migrations/`)
...

## Phase 2: Core Integration

### Step 3: Port the main component (`src/components/`)
...
````

Template guidance:
- Simple plans: use `## Main Phase` with `### Step N:` sections underneath.
- Complex plans: use multiple `## Phase N:` sections, each containing `### Step N:` steps. Step numbers are global (not per-phase).
- The flat `## Step N:` format (without phases) also works for backwards compatibility.
- Key invariants: one H1 title, one `## Overview`, numbered step sections (`### Step N:` or `## Step N:`), and at least one ordering section.

DB error in cloud mode raises loudly.\n   - **Hook-side op** `address_resolved_by_epic(epic_id, *, store, repo_root)` — both args required:\n     - `store` is the store that just persisted the transition (passed by the caller; never re-resolved). If `is_cloud_store(store)`, run the SQL update and use `store.load_codebase(epic.codebase_id).repo_workspace` as `repo_root` (the **epic's own codebase workspace**, not cwd) before walking files.\n     - File path: walk `tickets_dir(repo_root)`, flip frontmatter `status='addressed'` + `addressed_at` for any ticket whose `epics:` list contains `epic_id` with `resolves_on_complete: true` and `status=='open'`. Skip if `repo_root` is None or the directory does not exist (server-side DB-only deployment is valid).\n5. `_derive_source()` reads `MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID` (set → agent + filed_in_turn; unset → human); `MEGAPLAN_ACTOR_ID` populates `filed_by_actor_id` when present.\n6. Stdin body: `new(...)` accepts `body='-'` → `sys.stdin.read()`; rejects empty.\n\n## Phase 4: CLI + Hook + Wiring\n\n### Step 6: CLI subcommand group (`megaplan/cli.py`, `megaplan/handlers/tickets.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1. Register `ticket` subparser (mirror `epic` block at `cli.py:1256`): `new`, `list`, `show`, `edit`, `link`, `unlink`, `addressed`, `dismiss`, `reopen` with documented flags. Body source `-b BODY | --edit | -` is a required mutually-exclusive group; `-` reads `sys.stdin.read()` and rejects empty.\n2. `megaplan/handlers/tickets.py` — one handler per verb. `ticket new` writes only ULID + `\\n` to stdout.\n3. Wire dispatch in `cli.py` router.\n\n### Step 7: Auto-address hook — single surface in `record_epic_event` (`megaplan/store/db.py:2295`, `megaplan/store/file.py` equivalent)\n**Scope:** Small\n1. **Single hook location**: `record_epic_event`. After the event row is persisted, if `event_type == \"state_change\" AND post_state.get('state') == 'done'`, call:\n   ```python\n   from megaplan.tickets.core import address_resolved_by_epic\n   try:\n       cb = self.load_codebase(self.load_epic(epic_id).codebase_id) if isinstance(self, DBStore) else None\n       repo_root = cb.repo_workspace if cb else None\n       address_resolved_by_epic(epic_id, store=self, repo_root=repo_root)\n   except Exception:\n       # log, don't break the transition\n       ...\n   ```\n   - The store passed is **`self`** (the same store that recorded the event) — no `_resolve_store()` recursion.\n   - `repo_root` is derived from `codebases.repo_workspace` for the epic's `codebase_id` — not `os.getcwd()`. In server/multi-repo deployments this targets the correct on-disk repo, or skips file updates cleanly when `repo_workspace` is unset (DB-only deployment).\n2. **Why this is the right and only surface**: editorial gating drives terminal transitions through `record_event` → `store.record_epic_event` with `event_type=\"state_change\"` (verified at `gating.py:216`). `update_epic` callers that want a transition recorded already call `record_epic_event` after; we deliberately do **not** add a second hook in `update_epic` to avoid double-fire (FLAG `scope`). If a future caller mutates `state` via `update_epic` without recording an event, that's a pre-existing inconsistency outside this sprint.\n3. **Audit**: Step 1.2 confirmed `event_type='state_change'` is the only gating value emitted at terminal transitions; Step 1 also confirms `multi.py`/`compat.py` wrappers forward `record_epic_event` transparently. If audit reveals any wrapper that intercepts and reshapes `event_type` or `post_state`, normalize there.\n4. Idempotency comes from `address_resolved_by_epic` itself.\n\n### Step 8: Planner discovery integration (`megaplan/handlers/plan.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n1. Identify the new/refined-epic context-assembly function.\n2. Add an \"Open tickets for this repo\" section (top 20) ranked **strictly per the brief**: `tag overlap with epic GOAL desc, created_at desc` — overlap source is the epic goal text only. Cloud → `store.list_tickets(codebase_id, status='open')`; local-only → `megaplan.tickets.list_open(repo_root)`.\n3. Prompt mentions the planner can propose links with `resolves_on_complete=true`; actual linking via the explicit CLI.\n\n### Step 9: Agent env wiring + agent prompt (`megaplan/workers.py`, `megaplan/data/instructions.md`)\n**Scope:** Small\n1. **Inject `MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID` (and `MEGAPLAN_ACTOR_ID` when known) in `_codex_child_env` (`workers.py:632`) and `_external_worker_env` (`workers.py:642`)** — these are the env-builder functions feeding `subprocess.run(... env=env)` at `workers.py:245,284` for claude/codex/hermes subprocesses. Read the values from the worker context (the worker call sites have access to the active turn/actor; thread them through if not already in `os.environ`). This is the **only** place env injection is meaningful — `megaplan/resident/runtime.py` is in-process async and was the wrong target in v2.\n2. Add a short paragraph to `megaplan/data/instructions.md`: when to use `megaplan ticket new`; ULID printed to stdout.\n\n## Phase 5: Tests + Validation\n\n### Step 10: Tests (`tests/test_tickets.py`, `tests/test_tickets_cli.py`, extend `tests/test_cli_entry.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1. **Local-only mode**: `new`, `new -b -` (stdin), `list`, `show`, `edit`, `link`/`unlink`, status transitions, `address_resolved_by_epic` walks files idempotently.\n2. **Cloud-configured mode** (DBStore fixture): same ops do file + DB write-through; `link --resolves` writes both.\n3. **Codebase auto-registration**: first `ticket new` in a fresh repo creates a `codebases` row with `root_commit_sha` populated **NON-NULL**.\n4. **Auto-address hook (single fire)**: drive an epic to `done` via the editorial-gating path that emits `record_epic_event(event_type='state_change', post_state={'state':'done'})`; verify both file (in `repo_workspace`) and DB flip; call `record_epic_event` a second time → no-op (idempotent SQL filter); call `record_epic_event` with `event_type='state_change'` but `post_state={'state':'in_progress'}` → no fire; call with a non-state-change `event_type` and `post_state.state='done'` → no fire.\n5. **Multi-repo correctness**: with two `codebases` rows pointing at two different `repo_workspace` paths, drive epic A to `done` and verify only the `repo_workspace_A/.megaplan/tickets/` files are touched (NOT `os.getcwd()`).\n6. **Server-only deployment**: `repo_workspace=None` → SQL updates fire, file walk is skipped cleanly (no error).\n7. **Identity**: rename case (same root SHA → same codebase_id); Register `ticket` subparser (mirror `epic` block at `cli.py:1256`): all verbs from the design doc; body source `-b BODY | --edit | -` is required mutually exclusive; `-` reads `sys.stdin.read()` and rejects empty.\n2. `megaplan/handlers/tickets.py` — one handler per verb. `ticket new` writes only ULID + newline to stdout.\n3. Wire dispatch in `cli.py` router.\n\n### Step 7: Auto-address hook — single surface in `record_epic_event` (`megaplan/store/db.py:2295`, `megaplan/store/file.py` equivalent)\n**Scope:** Small\n1. **Single hook location**: `record_epic_event`. After the event row persists, if `event_type == \"state_change\"` AND `post_state.get('state') == 'done'`, derive `cb = self.load_codebase(self.load_epic(epic_id).codebase_id)`, set `repo_root = cb.repo_workspace if cb else None`, and call `address_resolved_by_epic(epic_id, store=self, repo_root=repo_root)`. Wrap in try/except so a hook failure logs but does not break the transition.\n2. **Why one surface**: editorial gating drives terminal transitions through `record_event` -> `store.record_epic_event` with `event_type='state_change'` (verified `gating.py:216`). We deliberately do NOT add a parallel hook in `update_epic` to avoid double-fire. If a future caller mutates `state` via `update_epic` without recording an event, that is a pre-existing inconsistency outside this sprint.\n3. **Audit**: Step 1.2 confirms `event_type='state_change'` is the canonical value; Step 1 confirms `multi.py`/`compat.py` wrappers forward `record_epic_event` transparently. If audit reveals reshape, normalize there.\n4. Idempotency: `address_resolved_by_epic` itself.\n\n### Step 8: Planner discovery integration (`megaplan/handlers/plan.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n1. Identify the new/refined-epic context-assembly function.\n2. Add an \"Open tickets for this repo\" section (top 20) ranked **strictly per the brief**: `tag overlap with epic GOAL desc, created_at desc` — overlap source is the epic goal text only.\n3. Prompt mentions the planner can propose links with `resolves_on_complete=true`; actual linking via the explicit CLI.\n\n### Step 9: Agent env wiring + agent prompt (`megaplan/workers.py`, `megaplan/data/instructions.md`)\n**Scope:** Small\n1. **Inject `MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID` (and `MEGAPLAN_ACTOR_ID` when known) in `_codex_child_env` (`workers.py:632`) and `_external_worker_env` (`workers.py:642`)** — these env-builder funcs feed `subprocess.run(... env=env)` at `workers.py:245,284`. If turn_id/actor_id are not already in `os.environ`, thread them through as params from the worker call sites or via a contextvar populated upstream. **This is the only place env injection is meaningful**; resident/runtime.py is in-process async and was the wrong target in v2.\n2. Add a short paragraph to `megaplan/data/instructions.md`: when to use `megaplan ticket new`; ULID printed to stdout.\n\n## Phase 5: Tests + Validation\n\n### Step 10: Tests (`tests/test_tickets.py`, `tests/test_tickets_cli.py`, extend `tests/test_cli_entry.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1. Local-only mode: `new`, `new -b -` (stdin), `list`, `show`, `edit`, `link`/`unlink`, status transitions, `address_resolved_by_epic` walks files idempotently.\n2. Cloud-configured mode (DBStore fixture): same ops do file + DB write-through; `link --resolves` writes both.\n3. Codebase auto-registration: first `ticket new` in a fresh repo creates a `codebases` row with `root_commit_sha` populated **NON-NULL**.\n4. Auto-address hook (single fire): drive an epic to `done` via `record_epic_event(event_type='state_change', post_state={'state':'done'})`; verify file (under `repo_workspace`) AND DB flip; second call no-op; `event_type='state_change'` with `post_state.state='in_progress'` -> no fire; non-state-change event_type with `post_state.state='done'` -> no fire.\n5. Server-only: `repo_workspace=None` -> SQL fires, file walk skipped cleanly.\n7. Identity: rename case (same root SHA -> same codebase_id); fresh-repo case errors cleanly.\n8.         Idea:
# Sprint brief — Tickets MVP

## Goal

Implement the tickets feature in megaplan per the design in `docs/tickets.md`. Tickets are short, repo-scoped notes on issues/problems, captured by humans or by agents on a human's prompt, that get folded into epics and auto-addressed when the resolving epic completes.

**The design doc `docs/tickets.md` is the source of truth for the data model, CLI surface, file format, modes, codebase identity, and integration touchpoints. Read it first. This brief only adds sprint scoping — what's in, what's out, what success looks like.**

## In scope for this sprint

Everything required to make tickets usable end-to-end in both **local-only mode** and **cloud-configured mode**:

1. **Schema migration (cloud-only)**: add `tickets` and `ticket_epics` tables; add `root_commit_sha` column to `codebases` with an index. Additive only — no changes to existing tables beyond the new column.
2. **`megaplan/store/db.py`**: CRUD helpers for the new tables, column allowlist entries, codebase resolution that prefers `root_commit_sha`.
3. **New module `megaplan/tickets/`**: the canonical local representation. Frontmatter read/write for `.megaplan/tickets/{ulid}-{slug}.md` files. Mode-aware dispatch (cloud-configured → file + DB; local-only → file only). Codebase identity resolution from cwd via `git rev-list --max-parents=0 HEAD`.
4. **CLI `megaplan ticket …`** with all verbs in `docs/tickets.md` § CLI: `new`, `list`, `show`, `edit`, `link`, `unlink`, `addressed`, `dismiss`, `reopen`. `ticket new` prints only the ULID to stdout on success; `--json` on read commands. `source` auto-derived from `MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID` env.
5. **Auto-address hook**: in the epic state-transition path, after an epic moves to `done`, call `megaplan.tickets.address_resolved_by_epic(epic_id)`. Implementation handles both modes per the design doc. Idempotent.
6. **Planner discovery integration**: when assembling context for new/refined epics, include open tickets for the current repo, ranked by `tag overlap with epic goal desc, created_at desc`. The planner can propose links with `resolves_on_complete=true`. Same data shape in both modes (DB query in cloud, file scan in local-only).
7. **Resident/agent prompt**: a short paragraph teaching the agent that `megaplan ticket new` exists and when to use it (out-of-scope observations during epic work, user-requested captures).
8. **Tests** covering both modes for: file ↔ DB round-trip, link/unlink, auto-address idempotence, codebase identity resolution (rename case, fresh-repo case), CLI happy paths.

## Out of scope (defer)

- **Offline queue / `.pending/` directory / `megaplan ticket sync`**. Write-through only for now; if the DB write fails in cloud mode, the CLI errors loudly and the user retries. The design doc describes the offline queue — explicitly **do not build it** this sprint. Build the minimal write-through path.
- Conflict detection on concurrent edits — last-write-wins is fine.
- Severity, assignment, comments, cross-repo tickets — explicitly excluded per the design doc.
- Anything described in the doc's **Open items** section.

## Success criteria

- `megaplan ticket new "title" -b "body"` works in a local-only repo and writes the `.md` file.
- The same command in a cloud-configured repo writes the `.md` file **and** inserts a `tickets` row, auto-registering the codebase if needed (with `root_commit_sha` populated).
- `megaplan ticket link <ticket> <epic> --resolves` updates both the file frontmatter and the DB join row.
- Marking an epic `done` flips the linked ticket's status to `addressed` and writes a `resolution_note` referencing the epic — in both modes.
- New epic creation surfaces open tickets for the repo in the planner's context, ranked.
- Tests pass for both modes.

## Notes for the planner

- Follow the **core-first principle**: `megaplan/tickets/` owns the capability; the DB layer is purely additive and only kicks in when a store is configured. Mode detection follows whatever mechanism megaplan core already uses (presence of a configured store) — do not invent a new one.
- The `tickets` concept is **distinct from `feedback`**. Do not reuse the `feedback` table; do not conflate their lifecycles.
- Vendor flags / FK conventions / migration patterns should match how existing tables are handled in `megaplan/store/db.py`. Read existing CRUD helpers before designing new ones.
- The auto-address hook integration point is the one place where you must touch existing epic state-transition code. Find the existing hook surface (where epic events are emitted on state change) and add the call there — don't invent a parallel transition path.

        Current plan (markdown):
        # Implementation Plan: Tickets MVP

## Overview
Add a repo-scoped tickets feature per `docs/tickets.md`. Build a new `megaplan/tickets/` module that owns the canonical local representation, wire mode-aware dispatch into the DB store, register a `megaplan ticket ...` CLI subcommand group, and inject one auto-address call into the place where epic state actually transitions to `done`. DB changes are additive: new `tickets` and `ticket_epics` tables plus `root_commit_sha` on `codebases`. No `.pending/` queue this sprint.

Verified repo facts:
- `update_epic` (`db.py:584-614`, `file.py:729-744`) is a generic UPDATE on arbitrary `**changes`; the column name is `state`.
- Editorial gating records terminal transitions via `record_epic_event` with **`event_type="state_change"`** (verified at `megaplan/editorial/gating.py:216`).
- `create_codebase`/`upsert_codebase` (`db.py:1797-1811`, `db.py:1830+`) hard-enumerate columns; `codebases.repo_workspace` already exists (allowlist `db.py:238`) and is the on-disk path for the repo.
- `MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID` is not exported anywhere today. Agent subprocess env is built in `megaplan/workers.py:_codex_child_env` (`workers.py:632`) and `_external_worker_env` (`workers.py:642`) — those funcs feed `subprocess.run(... env=env)` at `workers.py:245,284`. The resident runtime (`megaplan/resident/runtime.py`) is in-process async (no subprocess); env injection there does nothing.
- No canonical `cloud_configured` predicate exists; plan defines `is_cloud_store(store) := isinstance(store, DBStore)`.

Hook design (revised after v2 critique): the hook lives at exactly **one** surface — inside `record_epic_event`, gated on `event_type == "state_change" AND post_state.get('state') == 'done'`. The hook resolves `repo_root` from the epic's `codebases.repo_workspace` (not `os.getcwd()`), and uses `self` (the store recording the event) directly — no `_resolve_store()` recursion. Single fire per transition.

## Phase 1: Schema + Types

### Step 1: Audit (`megaplan/store/db.py`, `megaplan/store/file.py`, `megaplan/cli.py`, `megaplan/handlers/plan.py`, `megaplan/editorial/gating.py`, `megaplan/workers.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Read codebase CRUD (`db.py:1779-1955`) and allowlists (`db.py:237-238`); confirm `repo_workspace` column.
2. Confirm `record_epic_event` signature in both backends and `event_type='state_change'` from `gating.py:216`.
3. Locate `_codex_child_env` (`workers.py:632`) and `_external_worker_env` (`workers.py:642`) — env builders feeding `subprocess.run(... env=env)`.
4. Skim `cli.py:1256` (`epic` subparser).
5. Locate planner context-assembly site in `megaplan/handlers/plan.py`.

### Step 2: Schema migration (`supabase/migrations/2026MMDDHHMM_tickets.sql`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Additive: ALTER `codebases` ADD COLUMN `root_commit_sha text` + index; CREATE TABLE `tickets` and `ticket_epics` per design doc.
2. FK column types are explicitly **`text`** for `automation_actors.id`, `bot_turns.id`, `epics.id` references; `codebases.id` stays `uuid`.

### Step 3: Schema dataclasses (`megaplan/schemas/models.py`, `megaplan/schemas/__init__.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Add `Ticket` and `TicketEpicLink` models; export.
2. Add `root_commit_sha: str | None = None` to `Codebase`.

## Phase 2: DB Store

### Step 4: DB store CRUD (`megaplan/store/db.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Extend `_IDEMPOTENT_MUTATORS` (`db.py:89`) and exports (`db.py:114`).
2. Extend column allowlists (`db.py:237-238`): add `root_commit_sha` to `codebases`; add `tickets`/`ticket_epics`.
3. **Extend `create_codebase` and `upsert_codebase` INSERT SQL** (`db.py:1797-1811`, `db.py:1830+`) to add `root_commit_sha` to column list, VALUES, params, and signature. For `upsert_codebase`, add to `ON CONFLICT ... DO UPDATE SET`.
4. Implement: `create_ticket`, `load_ticket`, `list_tickets`, `update_ticket` (LWW), `link_ticket_to_epic` (INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE), `unlink_ticket_from_epic`, `list_ticket_epic_links`, `address_tickets_resolved_by_epic(epic_id) -> list[str]` (single SQL UPDATE filtered on `resolves_on_complete=true AND status='open'` RETURNING ids; idempotent).
5. `resolve_codebase_by_root_sha(*, root_sha, owner, name, repo_url, default_branch, repo_workspace=None) -> str`.

## Phase 3: Tickets Module

### Step 5: New module `megaplan/tickets/`
**Scope:** Medium
1. `megaplan/tickets/__init__.py` exports public API + `is_cloud_store`.
2. `megaplan/tickets/identity.py`: `repo_root_sha` via `git rev-list --max-parents=0 HEAD`; `repo_owner_name`; `repo_default_branch`.
3. `megaplan/tickets/files.py`: tickets dir, frontmatter via `pyyaml`, slugify, iterators.
4. `megaplan/tickets/core.py`:
   - **`is_cloud_store(store) -> bool := isinstance(store, DBStore)`** — canonical predicate.
   - `_resolve_store() -> Store | None` reuses megaplan's existing factory.
   - **CLI-side ops** call `_resolve_store()` once, write the file in cwd's repo, and if cloud also call the DB helper.
   - **Hook-side op** `address_resolved_by_epic(epic_id, *, store, repo_root)` — both args required. `store` is passed by the caller (never re-resolved). If cloud, run the SQL update. File path: walk `tickets_dir(repo_root)` (skip cleanly if `repo_root is None` or directory missing) and flip frontmatter idempotently.
5. `_derive_source()` reads `MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID` env; `MEGAPLAN_ACTOR_ID` populates `filed_by_actor_id`.
6. Stdin body: `new(...)` accepts `body='-'` -> `sys.stdin.read()`; rejects empty.

## Phase 4: CLI + Hook + Wiring

### Step 6: CLI subcommand group (`megaplan/cli.py`, `megaplan/handlers/tickets.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Register `ticket` subparser (mirror `epic` block at `cli.py:1256`): all verbs from the design doc; body source `-b BODY | --edit | -` is required mutually exclusive; `-` reads `sys.stdin.read()` and rejects empty.
2. `megaplan/handlers/tickets.py` — one handler per verb. `ticket new` writes only ULID + newline to stdout.
3. Wire dispatch in `cli.py` router.

### Step 7: Auto-address hook — single surface in `record_epic_event` (`megaplan/store/db.py:2295`, `megaplan/store/file.py` equivalent)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Single hook location**: `record_epic_event`. After the event row persists, if `event_type == "state_change"` AND `post_state.get('state') == 'done'`, derive `cb = self.load_codebase(self.load_epic(epic_id).codebase_id)`, set `repo_root = cb.repo_workspace if cb else None`, and call `address_resolved_by_epic(epic_id, store=self, repo_root=repo_root)`. Wrap in try/except so a hook failure logs but does not break the transition.
2. **Why one surface**: editorial gating drives terminal transitions through `record_event` -> `store.record_epic_event` with `event_type='state_change'` (verified `gating.py:216`). We deliberately do NOT add a parallel hook in `update_epic` to avoid double-fire. If a future caller mutates `state` via `update_epic` without recording an event, that is a pre-existing inconsistency outside this sprint.
3. **Audit**: Step 1.2 confirms `event_type='state_change'` is the canonical value; Step 1 confirms `multi.py`/`compat.py` wrappers forward `record_epic_event` transparently. If audit reveals reshape, normalize there.
4. Idempotency: `address_resolved_by_epic` itself.

### Step 8: Planner discovery integration (`megaplan/handlers/plan.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Identify the new/refined-epic context-assembly function.
2. Add an "Open tickets for this repo" section (top 20) ranked **strictly per the brief**: `tag overlap with epic GOAL desc, created_at desc` — overlap source is the epic goal text only.
3. Prompt mentions the planner can propose links with `resolves_on_complete=true`; actual linking via the explicit CLI.

### Step 9: Agent env wiring + agent prompt (`megaplan/workers.py`, `megaplan/data/instructions.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Inject `MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID` (and `MEGAPLAN_ACTOR_ID` when known) in `_codex_child_env` (`workers.py:632`) and `_external_worker_env` (`workers.py:642`)** — these env-builder funcs feed `subprocess.run(... env=env)` at `workers.py:245,284`. If turn_id/actor_id are not already in `os.environ`, thread them through as params from the worker call sites or via a contextvar populated upstream. **This is the only place env injection is meaningful**; resident/runtime.py is in-process async and was the wrong target in v2.
2. Add a short paragraph to `megaplan/data/instructions.md`: when to use `megaplan ticket new`; ULID printed to stdout.

## Phase 5: Tests + Validation

### Step 10: Tests (`tests/test_tickets.py`, `tests/test_tickets_cli.py`, extend `tests/test_cli_entry.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Local-only mode: `new`, `new -b -` (stdin), `list`, `show`, `edit`, `link`/`unlink`, status transitions, `address_resolved_by_epic` walks files idempotently.
2. Cloud-configured mode (DBStore fixture): same ops do file + DB write-through; `link --resolves` writes both.
3. Codebase auto-registration: first `ticket new` in a fresh repo creates a `codebases` row with `root_commit_sha` populated **NON-NULL**.
4. Auto-address hook (single fire): drive an epic to `done` via `record_epic_event(event_type='state_change', post_state={'state':'done'})`; verify file (under `repo_workspace`) AND DB flip; second call no-op; `event_type='state_change'` with `post_state.state='in_progress'` -> no fire; non-state-change event_type with `post_state.state='done'` -> no fire.
5. Multi-repo correctness: two `codebases` rows at different `repo_workspace` paths; transition for codebase A only touches A's `.megaplan/tickets/` (NOT `os.getcwd()`).
6. Server-only: `repo_workspace=None` -> SQL fires, file walk skipped cleanly.
7. Identity: rename case (same root SHA -> same codebase_id); fresh-repo case errors cleanly.
8. CLI happy paths via subprocess: `ticket new` stdout is exactly ULID + newline; `list --json` valid JSON; `link --resolves` round-trip.
9. Agent env: invoke the env-builders with a turn id in scope and assert `MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID` ends up in the subprocess env dict.

### Step 11: Validation
**Scope:** Small
1. `pytest tests/test_tickets.py tests/test_tickets_cli.py -x`.
2. `pytest tests/test_cli_entry.py tests/test_auto.py -x`.
3. `pytest -x`.
4. Smoke: scratch git repo with `repo_workspace` set, `MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID=t_test megaplan ticket new "test" -b "body" -t demo`; verify stdout is just the ULID; file has `source: agent`, `filed_in_turn: t_test`.

## Execution Order
1. Phase 1 (migration + models).
2. Phase 2 (DB CRUD + extended `create_codebase`/`upsert_codebase` INSERTs).
3. Phase 3 (`megaplan/tickets/` with `is_cloud_store` and explicit `store`+`repo_root` on `address_resolved_by_epic`).
4. Phase 4: CLI -> single hook in `record_epic_event` -> planner + worker env wiring.
5. Phase 5: tests + validation.

## Validation Order
1. Targeted ticket tests (incl. multi-repo and server-only cases).
2. CLI + epic-transition regression.
3. Full suite.
4. Manual smoke.

    "iteration": 3,
    "idea": "# Sprint brief \u2014 Tickets MVP\n\n## Goal\n\nImplement the tickets feature in megaplan per the design in `docs/tickets.md`. Tickets are short, repo-scoped notes on issues/problems, captured by humans or by agents on a human's prompt, that get folded into epics and auto-addressed when the resolving epic completes.\n\n**The design doc `docs/tickets.md` is the source of truth for the data model, CLI surface, file format, modes, codebase identity, and integration touchpoints. Read it first. This brief only adds sprint scoping \u2014 what's in, what's out, what success looks like.**\n\n## In scope for this sprint\n\nEverything required to make tickets usable end-to-end in both **local-only mode** and **cloud-configured mode**:\n\n1. **Schema migration (cloud-only)**: add `tickets` and `ticket_epics` tables; add `root_commit_sha` column to `codebases` with an index. Additive only \u2014 no changes to existing tables beyond the new column.\n2. **`megaplan/store/db.py`**: CRUD helpers for the new tables, column allowlist entries, codebase resolution that prefers `root_commit_sha`.\n3. **New module `megaplan/tickets/`**: the canonical local representation. Frontmatter read/write for `.megaplan/tickets/{ulid}-{slug}.md` files. Mode-aware dispatch (cloud-configured \u2192 file + DB; local-only \u2192 file only). Codebase identity resolution from cwd via `git rev-list --max-parents=0 HEAD`.\n4. **CLI `megaplan ticket \u2026`** with all verbs in `docs/tickets.md` \u00a7 CLI: `new`, `list`, `show`, `edit`, `link`, `unlink`, `addressed`, `dismiss`, `reopen`. `ticket new` prints only the ULID to stdout on success; `--json` on read commands. `source` auto-derived from `MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID` env.\n5. **Auto-address hook**: in the epic state-transition path, after an epic moves to `done`, call `megaplan.tickets.address_resolved_by_epic(epic_id)`. Implementation handles both modes per the design doc. Idempotent.\n6. **Planner discovery integration**: when assembling context for new/refined epics, include open tickets for the current repo, ranked by `tag overlap with epic goal desc, created_at desc`. The planner can propose links with `resolves_on_complete=true`. Same data shape in both modes (DB query in cloud, file scan in local-only).\n7. **Resident/agent prompt**: a short paragraph teaching the agent that `megaplan ticket new` exists and when to use it (out-of-scope observations during epic work, user-requested captures).\n8. **Tests** covering both modes for: file \u2194 DB round-trip, link/unlink, auto-address idempotence, codebase identity resolution (rename case, fresh-repo case), CLI happy paths.\n\n## Out of scope (defer)\n\n- **Offline queue / `.pending/` directory / `megaplan ticket sync`**. Write-through only for now; if the DB write fails in cloud mode, the CLI errors loudly and the user retries. The design doc describes the offline queue \u2014 explicitly **do not build it** this sprint. Build the minimal write-through path.\n- Conflict detection on concurrent edits \u2014 last-write-wins is fine.\n- Severity, assignment, comments, cross-repo tickets \u2014 explicitly excluded per the design doc.\n- Anything described in the doc's **Open items** section.\n\n## Success criteria\n\n- `megaplan ticket new \"title\" -b \"body\"` works in a local-only repo and writes the `.md` file.\n- The same command in a cloud-configured repo writes the `.md` file **and** inserts a `tickets` row, auto-registering the codebase if needed (with `root_commit_sha` populated).\n- `megaplan ticket link <ticket> <epic> --resolves` updates both the file frontmatter and the DB join row.\n- Marking an epic `done` flips the linked ticket's status to `addressed` and writes a `resolution_note` referencing the epic \u2014 in both modes.\n- New epic creation surfaces open tickets for the repo in the planner's context, ranked.\n- Tests pass for both modes.\n\n## Notes for the planner\n\n- Follow the **core-first principle**: `megaplan/tickets/` owns the capability; the DB layer is purely additive and only kicks in when a store is configured. Mode detection follows whatever mechanism megaplan core already uses (presence of a configured store) \u2014 do not invent a new one.\n- The `tickets` concept is **distinct from `feedback`**. Do not reuse the `feedback` table; do not conflate their lifecycles.\n- Vendor flags / FK conventions / migration patterns should match how existing tables are handled in `megaplan/store/db.py`. Read existing CRUD helpers before designing new ones.\n- The auto-address hook integration point is the one place where you must touch existing epic state-transition code. Find the existing hook surface (where epic events are emitted on state change) and add the call there \u2014 don't invent a parallel transition path.",

        Requirements:
        - Before addressing individual flags, check: does any flag suggest the plan is targeting the wrong code or the wrong root cause? If so, consider whether the plan needs a new approach rather than adjustments. Explain your reasoning.
        - Update the plan to address the significant issues.
        - Keep the plan readable and executable.
        - Return flags_addressed with the exact flag IDs you addressed.
        - Include `changes_summary` as a short plain-English summary of what changed in the revision. If there were no concrete flags, say that explicitly (for example: `No critique flags were raised; refined wording and kept the plan aligned for execution.`).
        - Preserve or improve success criteria quality. Each criterion must have a `priority` of `must`, `should`, or `info`. Promote or demote priorities if critique feedback reveals a criterion was over- or under-weighted.
        - Each success criterion should include a `requires` field listing the capabilities needed for verification. Valid capability strings: `run_shell`, `read_files`, `run_tests`, `parse_diff`, `read_build_output`, `run_linter` (container), `drive_browser`, `inspect_runtime_ui`, `observe_runtime_logs`, `subjective_judgment`, `verify_physical_device` (human). `must` criteria MUST have non-empty `requires`. Example: `{"criterion": "All tests pass", "priority": "must", "requires": ["run_tests"]}`.
        - Verify that the plan remains aligned with the user's original intent, not just internal plan quality.
        - Remove unjustified scope growth. If critique raised scope creep, narrow the plan back to the original idea unless the broader work is strictly required.
        - Maintain the structural template: H1 title, ## Overview, phase sections with numbered step sections, ## Execution Order or ## Validation Order.
        - CRITICAL: Your entire revised plan markdown (all sections) must be output as the `plan` field in the structured output. The prose response must not contain the plan text.
        - CRITICAL: Return only the structured JSON object for the schema fields `plan`, `changes_summary`, `flags_addressed`, `assumptions`, `success_criteria`, and `questions`. Do not add commentary before or after the JSON object.

        Plan template — simple format (adapt to the actual repo and scope):
````md
# Implementation Plan: [Title]

## Overview
Summarize the goal, current repository shape, and the constraints that matter.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Audit the current behavior (`megaplan/prompts.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Inspect** the current implementation and call out the exact insertion points (`megaplan/prompts.py:29`).

### Step 2: Add the first change (`megaplan/evaluation.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** the smallest viable change with exact file references (`megaplan/evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Capture** any tricky behavior with a short example.
   ```python
   issues = validate_plan_structure(plan_text)
   ```

### Step 3: Wire downstream behavior (`megaplan/handlers.py`, `megaplan/workers.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Update** the runtime flow in the touched files (`megaplan/handlers.py:400`, `megaplan/workers.py:199`).

### Step 4: Prove the change (`tests/test_evaluation.py`, `tests/test_megaplan.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** the cheapest targeted checks first (`tests/test_evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Finish** with broader verification once the wiring is in place (`tests/test_megaplan.py:1`).

## Execution Order
1. Update prompts and mocks before enforcing stricter validation.
2. Land higher-risk wiring after the validator and tests are ready.

## Validation Order
1. Start with focused unit tests.
2. Run the broader suite after the flow changes are in place.
````

For complex plans, use multiple phases:
````md
## Phase 1: Foundation — Dependencies, DB, Types

### Step 1: Install dependencies (`package.json`)
...

### Step 2: Create database migration (`supabase/migrations/`)
...

## Phase 2: Core Integration

### Step 3: Port the main component (`src/components/`)
...
````

Template guidance:
- Simple plans: use `## Main Phase` with `### Step N:` sections underneath.
- Complex plans: use multiple `## Phase N:` sections, each containing `### Step N:` steps. Step numbers are global (not per-phase).
- The flat `## Step N:` format (without phases) also works for backwards compatibility.
- Key invariants: one H1 title, one `## Overview`, numbered step sections (`### Step N:` or `## Step N:`), and at least one ordering section.

Build a new `megaplan/tickets/` module that owns the canonical local representation, wire mode-aware dispatch into the DB store, register a `megaplan ticket ...` CLI subcommand group, and inject one auto-address call into the canonical epic state-transition surface. DB changes are additive: new `tickets` and `ticket_epics` tables plus `root_commit_sha` on `codebases`. No `.pending/` queue this sprint.\n\nVerified repo facts (corrected from v3):\n- `update_epic` (`db.py:584-614`, `file.py:729-744`) is a generic UPDATE on arbitrary `**changes`; column is `state`.\n- Editorial gating records terminal transitions via `record_epic_event` with `event_type=\"state_change\"` (verified `megaplan/editorial/gating.py:216`).\n- **`epics` has NO `codebase_id` column** (allowlist `db.py:247`). The relationship is reverse: `codebases.associated_epic_id` (allowlist `db.py:238`); existing reverse-lookup pattern at `db.py:2713`: `SELECT * FROM codebases WHERE associated_epic_id = %s`.\n- `codebases.repo_workspace` is the on-disk path; `codebases` is a DB-only concept (FileStore has no `load_codebase`; `FileStore.record_epic_event` is at `file.py:1292`).\n- `create_codebase`/`upsert_codebase` (`db.py:1797-1811`, `db.py:1830+`) hard-enumerate columns.\n- `MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID` is not exported anywhere today. Subprocess env is built in `_codex_child_env` (`workers.py:632`) and `_external_worker_env` (`workers.py:642`); subprocess.run sites are `workers.py:238, 1773, 1978`. `workers.py` has zero `turn_id` in scope today — `bot_turns.id` is a resident-runtime concept that is NOT available to plan-phase workers.\n- No canonical `cloud_configured` predicate; we define `is_cloud_store(store) := isinstance(store, DBStore)`.\n\nThree concrete corrections in this revision:\n1. **Reverse-direction codebase lookup**: derive `repo_root` from `SELECT * FROM codebases WHERE associated_epic_id = epic_id` (zero or one match; skip cleanly if zero or `repo_workspace` is NULL).\n2. **FileStore hook is a no-op file walk**: in `FileStore.record_epic_event` we still call the helper but pass `repo_root=` derived from the epic's on-disk location (`<file-store-root>/.megaplan/tickets/`); we do NOT attempt `load_codebase` on FileStore. There is no DB SQL update in this path. The hook still fires; it just does the file-only path.\n3. **`MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID` value source committed**: in plan-phase workers we set a **synthetic marker** `plan_worker_{plan_id}` (or the active plan/epic id available at the call site). The brief's contract — `source='agent'` flips on when the var is set — is satisfied. `filed_in_turn_id` carries the synthetic string in plan-phase contexts; the column is `text` and FK validation is deferred to the resident-runtime path where real `bot_turns.id` IS available (resident is out of scope for env injection because it's in-process — but the CLI call from a real resident-driven subprocess can still set MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID itself; we only need to handle the plan-phase worker case here).\n\n## Phase 1: Schema + Types\n\n### Step 1: Audit (`megaplan/store/db.py`, `megaplan/store/file.py`, `megaplan/cli.py`, `megaplan/handlers/plan.py`, `megaplan/editorial/gating.py`, `megaplan/workers.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n1. The brief's contract — `source='agent'` flips on when the var is set — is satisfied. `filed_in_turn_id` is declared `text` WITHOUT a strict FK to bot_turns so the synthetic marker stores cleanly; real `bot_turns.id` values still work when a CLI invocation inherits MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID from a resident-driven environment.\n\n## Phase 1: Schema + Types\n\n### Step 1: Audit (`megaplan/store/db.py`, `megaplan/store/file.py`, `megaplan/cli.py`, `megaplan/handlers/plan.py`, `megaplan/editorial/gating.py`, `megaplan/workers.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n1. Read codebase CRUD (`db.py:1779-1955`) and allowlists (`db.py:237-247`); confirm `repo_workspace` and `associated_epic_id` columns; confirm `epics` has no `codebase_id`.\n2. Verify `record_epic_event` signature in both backends (`db.py:2295`, `file.py:1292`); confirm `event_type='state_change'` from `gating.py:216`.\n3. Locate `_codex_child_env` (`workers.py:632`) and `_external_worker_env` (`workers.py:642`); confirm subprocess.run sites at `workers.py:238, 1773, 1978`. Identify which id (plan_id, epic_id, phase id) IS in scope at each call site to use as the synthetic turn marker.\n4. Skim `cli.py:1256` (`epic` subparser).\n5. Locate planner context-assembly site in `megaplan/handlers/plan.py`.\n6. Confirm FileStore working-tree root attribute (`self._root` or equivalent).\n\n### Step 2: Schema migration (`supabase/migrations/2026MMDDHHMM_tickets.sql`)\n**Scope:** Small\n1. Additive: ALTER `codebases` ADD COLUMN `root_commit_sha text` + index; CREATE TABLE `tickets`, CREATE TABLE `ticket_epics` per design doc.\n2. FK column types: `text` for `automation_actors.id` and `epics.id` references; `codebases.id` stays `uuid`. **`filed_in_turn_id text` declared WITHOUT FK to `bot_turns(id)`** so plan-phase synthetic markers store cleanly. Document why in the migration comment.\n\n### Step 3: Schema dataclasses (`megaplan/schemas/models.py`, `megaplan/schemas/__init__.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n1. Add `Ticket` and `TicketEpicLink`; export.\n2. Extend column allowlists: add `root_commit_sha` to `codebases`; add `tickets`/`ticket_epics`.\n3. **Extend `create_codebase` and `upsert_codebase` INSERT SQL** (`db.py:1797-1811`, `db.py:1830+`) to include `root_commit_sha` in column list, VALUES, params, signature; for `upsert_codebase` add to `ON CONFLICT ... DO UPDATE SET`.\n4. Implement: `create_ticket`, `load_ticket`, `list_tickets`, `update_ticket` (LWW), `link_ticket_to_epic` (INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE), `unlink_ticket_from_epic`, `list_ticket_epic_links`, `address_tickets_resolved_by_epic(epic_id) -> list[str]` (SQL UPDATE filtered on `resolves_on_complete=true AND status='open'` RETURNING ids).\n5. **`load_codebase_by_associated_epic(epic_id) -> Codebase | None`** — new helper using existing reverse-lookup pattern (`db.py:2713`): `SELECT * FROM codebases WHERE associated_epic_id = %s`. Returns at most one row; None if no match.\n6. `resolve_codebase_by_root_sha(*, root_sha, owner, name, repo_url, default_branch, repo_workspace=None) -> str`.\n\n## Phase 3: Tickets Module\n\n### Step 5: New module `megaplan/tickets/`\n**Scope:** Medium\n1. `megaplan/tickets/__init__.py` exports public API + `is_cloud_store`.\n2. `megaplan/tickets/identity.py`: `repo_root_sha`, `repo_owner_name`, `repo_default_branch`.\n3. `megaplan/tickets/files.py`: tickets dir, frontmatter via `pyyaml`, slugify, iterators.\n4. `megaplan/tickets/core.py`:\n   - `is_cloud_store(store) -> bool := isinstance(store, DBStore)` — canonical predicate.\n   - `_resolve_store() -> Store | None` reuses megaplan's existing factory.\n   - CLI-side ops call `_resolve_store()` once, write the file in cwd's repo, and if cloud also call the DB helper.\n   - **Hook-side op** `address_resolved_by_epic(epic_id, *, store, repo_root)` — `store` and `repo_root` both supplied by caller. If `is_cloud_store(store)`, run the SQL update. File path: walk `tickets_dir(repo_root)` if `repo_root` is set and the dir exists; else skip cleanly. Idempotent.\n5. `_derive_source()` reads `MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID` env (set → agent + filed_in_turn; unset → human); `MEGAPLAN_ACTOR_ID` populates `filed_by_actor_id`.\n6. Wire dispatch in `cli.py` router.\n\n### Step 7: Auto-address hook — single surface in `record_epic_event`\n**Scope:** Small\n1. **In `DBStore.record_epic_event` (`db.py:2295`)**: after the event row persists, if `event_type == \"state_change\"` AND `post_state.get('state') == 'done'`:\n   ```python\n   from megaplan.tickets.core import address_resolved_by_epic\n   cb = self.load_codebase_by_associated_epic(epic_id)  # new helper, Step 4.5\n   repo_root = cb.repo_workspace if (cb and cb.repo_workspace) else None\n   address_resolved_by_epic(epic_id, store=self, repo_root=repo_root)\n   ```\n   - **No try/except wrapper** — let real bugs surface in tests. The helper is idempotent and safe.\n   - If no associated codebase exists, `cb` is None and `repo_root` is None: SQL UPDATE still fires; file walk is skipped.\n2. **In `FileStore.record_epic_event` (`file.py:1292`)**: same gate. Pass `repo_root = self._root` (or whatever attribute holds the FileStore root, audited in Step 1.6). **Do NOT call `load_codebase_by_associated_epic`** — codebases are a DB concept and FileStore has no such method. The DB SQL path is a no-op when `is_cloud_store(self)` is False; only the file walk runs.\n3. **Why one surface**: editorial gating drives terminal transitions through `record_event` → `store.record_epic_event` with `event_type='state_change'`. Step 1.3 audits which id is most natural at each site.\n2. **Why a synthetic marker is sufficient**: the brief's contract is `source='agent'` whenever `MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID` is set — a boolean signal. `filed_in_turn_id` is plain text (no FK, Step 2.2), so the synthetic marker stores cleanly. Real `bot_turns.id` values flow when the CLI is invoked from a resident-driven path that inherits the env.\n3. Add a short paragraph to `megaplan/data/instructions.md`: when to use `megaplan ticket new`; ULID printed to stdout.\n\n## Phase 5: Tests + Validation\n\n### Step 10: Tests (`tests/test_tickets.py`, `tests/test_tickets_cli.py`, extend `tests/test_cli_entry.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1. Plan v4 fixes the three concrete bugs from v3: reverse-direction codebase lookup via new `load_codebase_by_associated_epic` helper (db.py:2713 pattern), explicit FileStore handling using `self._root` with no `load_codebase` call, and a committed synthetic-marker strategy (`plan_worker_{plan_id}`) for `MEGAPLAN_TURN_ID` with `filed_in_turn_id` declared as plain text without a strict FK.
